1. **Identify the black nodes:**
   - Node J
   - Node E

2. **Find the immediate neighboring nodes of J and E:**
   - Node J is connected to Node D.
   - Node E is connected to Node B and Node L.

3. **Identify paths from J to E:**
   - Path 1: J -> D -> P -> O -> N -> B -> E
   - Path 2: J -> D -> P -> O -> Y -> C -> H -> I
   - Path 3: J -> D -> P -> O -> N -> B -> F -> I
   - Path 4: J -> D -> P -> O -> N -> B -> L -> E
   - Path 5: J -> D -> P -> O -> Y -> C -> H -> G -> X -> K

4. **Identify critical nodes:**
   - To disconnect J and E, we need to remove nodes that are common in multiple paths.
   - Node P is a critical node as it appears in multiple paths connecting J and E.
   - Node O is also a critical node as it appears in multiple paths connecting J and E.
   - Node N is another critical node as it appears in multiple paths connecting J and E.

5. **Evaluate the removal of nodes:**
   - Removing nodes P, O, and N will disconnect all paths between J and E.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes J and E is:

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [P, O, N]

1. **Identify the black nodes:** The black nodes are G and N.
2. **Find all paths between G and N:**
   - G is connected to H, E, and X.
   - N is connected to C, O, and Y.
   - H is connected to B, I.
   - B is connected to C, I.
   - E is connected to L, M, and I.
   - I is connected to J, P.
   - L is connected to M.
   - M is connected to J.
   - C is connected to O.
   - O is connected to P.
   - Y is connected to P.

   By tracing these connections, we can see the following paths from G to N:
   - G -> H -> B -> C -> N
   - G -> H -> B -> C -> O -> N
   - G -> H -> I -> P -> O -> N
   - G -> H -> I -> P -> O -> N
   - G -> E -> I -> B -> C -> N
   - G -> E -> I -> B -> C -> O -> N
   - G -> E -> I -> P -> O -> N

3. **Identify the edges that need to be removed to disconnect G and N:**
   - Removing the edge (H, B) will disconnect the path G -> H -> B -> C -> N.
   - Removing the edge (I, P) will disconnect the path G -> E -> I -> P -> O -> N.
   - Removing the edge (C, N) will disconnect the remaining path G -> H -> B -> C -> N, if not already disconnected.

By removing these three edges, all paths between G and N are broken. No fewer edges can achieve this disconnection because each of these edges is part of at least one unique path between G and N.

**Output Format:**

Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(H, B), (I, P), (C, N)].
